    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.

    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.

    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

  SELECT c.Segment, y.CustomerID, SUM(y.Consumption) AS total_consumption
  FROM yearmonth y
  JOIN customers c ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
  WHERE c.Currency = 'CZK'
    AND substr(y.Date, 1, 4) = '2013'
  GROUP BY c.Segment, y.CustomerID
),
min_by_seg AS (
  SELECT Segment, MIN(total_consumption) AS min_consumption
  FROM seg_totals
  GROUP BY Segment
),
cnt_min AS (
  SELECT s.Segment, COUNT(*) AS cnt_lowest
  FROM seg_totals s
  JOIN min_by_seg m
    ON s.Segment = m.Segment
   AND s.total_consumption = m.min_consumption
  GROUP BY s.Segment
),
seg_year_total AS (
  SELECT c.Segment, SUM(y.Consumption) AS total_year_consumption
  FROM yearmonth y
  JOIN customers c ON y.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
  WHERE c.Currency = 'CZK'
    AND substr(y.Date, 1, 4) = '2013'
  GROUP BY c.Segment
),
avg_cons AS (
  SELECT t.Segment, t.total_year_consumption * 1.0 / c.cnt_lowest AS annual_avg
  FROM seg_year_total t
  JOIN cnt_min c ON t.Segment = c.Segment
)
SELECT 
  (SELECT annual_avg FROM avg_cons WHERE Segment = 'SME') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM avg_cons WHERE Segment = 'LAM'),
  (SELECT annual_avg FROM avg_cons WHERE Segment = 'LAM') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM avg_cons WHERE Segment = 'KAM'),
  (SELECT annual_avg FROM avg_cons WHERE Segment = 'KAM') - (SELECT annual_avg FROM avg_cons WHERE Segment = 'SME');
"""))

``````python
print(db.execute("""
WITH seg AS (
  SELECT c.Segment,
         SUM(CASE WHEN substr(ym.Date,1,4)='2012' THEN ym.Consumption ELSE 0 END) AS c2012,
         SUM(CASE WHEN substr(ym.Date,1,4)='2013' THEN ym.Consumption ELSE 0 END) AS c2013
  FROM yearmonth ym
  JOIN customers c ON c.CustomerID = ym.CustomerID
  WHERE c.Segment IN ('SME','LAM','KAM')
    AND c.Currency = 'EUR'
    AND substr(ym.Date,1,4) IN ('2012','2013')
  GROUP BY c.Segment
)
SELECT Segment,
       (c2013 - c2012) * 100.0 / c2013 AS pct_increase
FROM seg
ORDER BY pct_increase DESC;
"""))

    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

    metric column itsel

  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

```python
print(db.execute("""
SELECT (y2012.consumption - y2013.consumption) * 1.0 / y2012.consumption AS decrease_rate
FROM (
  SELECT CustomerID
  FROM transactions_1k
  WHERE Date = '2012-08-25'
    AND Price = 634.8
) c
JOIN (
  SELECT CustomerID, SUM(Consumption) AS consumption
  FROM yearmonth
  WHERE Date LIKE '2012%'
  GROUP BY CustomerID
) y2012 ON y2012.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
JOIN (
  SELECT CustomerID, SUM(Consumption) AS consumption
  FROM yearmonth
  WHERE Date LIKE '2013%'
  GROUP BY CustomerID
) y2013 ON y2013.CustomerID = c.CustomerID
"""))
```

      "September 2013 refers to 201309"  → WHERE date_col = '201309'  (not LIKE '2013-09%')
      "ratio = count(A) / count(B)"      → SELECT COUNT(CASE WHEN x='A' THEN 1 END)*1.0 / COUNT(CASE WHEN x='B' THEN 1 END)
      "meeting events refers to type = 'Meeting'" → WHERE type = 'Meeting'
  ② If any names, locations, or string values are NOT defined by the Hint,
    use db.sample_values() to see how they are actually stored in the database.

  ```python

  ```

Turn 3 — FINALIZE (plain text only, never inside a code block):
  FINAL("YOUR SQL HERE")

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.

  Q: Calculate the difference between the total number of students and the number of international international students in Univeristy of Tokyo from 2011 to 2014.
  Hint: international students refers to DIVIDE(MULTIPLY(num_students, pct_international_students), 100); difference refers to SUBTRACT(SUM(num_students), SUM(DIVIDE(MULTIPLY(num_students, pct_international_students), 100))); in University of Tokyo refers to university_name = 'University of Tokyo'; from 2011 to 2014 refers to year BETWEEN 2011 AND 2014

  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.

    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).

    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.

    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.

    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.

    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.

    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.

    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

  'Business' is a major name; wear medium size t-shirt refers to t_shirt_size = 'Medium'

  Q: How many times is the number of "Women's Clothing" Yelp businesses to "Men's Clothing"?
  Hint: "Women's Clothing" Yelp businesses refers to  category_name = 'Women''s Clothing'; "Men's Clothing" refers to category_name = 'Men''s Clothing'; times refers to DIVIDE(COUNT(category_name = 'Women''s Clothing'), COUNT(category_name = 'Men''s Clothing'))
  SQL: SELECT CAST(SUM(CASE WHEN T2.category_name = 'Women''s Clothing' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS REAL) / SUM(CASE WHEN T2.category_name = 'Men''s Clothing' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS TIMES FROM Business_Categories AS T1 INNER JOIN Categories AS T2 ON T1.category_id = T2.category_id

    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.

    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.

    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.

    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

    metric column itsel

QUESTION: What is the ratio of customers who pay in EUR against customers who pay in CZK?
⚠️  HINT (follow these definitions EXACTLY):
  ratio of customers who pay in EUR against customers who pay in CZK = count(Currency = 'EUR') / count(Currency = 'CZK').

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: Among the customers in Asia, how many customers are in debt?
  Hint: customers in Asia refer to n_name where r_name = 'ASIA'; customers in debt refer to c_acctbal < 0;
  SQL: SELECT COUNT(T1.n_name) FROM nation AS T1 INNER JOIN customer AS T2 ON T1.n_nationkey = T2.c_nationkey INNER JOIN region AS T3 ON T1.n_regionkey = T3.r_regionkey WHERE T2.c_acctbal < 0 AND T3.r_name = 'ASIA'

Schema:
DATABASE: debit_card_specializing

TABLE: customers
  CustomerID (INTEGER) [PK, NOT NULL]
  Segment (TEXT)  e.g. 'SME', 'LAM'
  Currency (TEXT)  e.g. 'EUR', 'CZK'

TABLE: gasstations
  GasStationID (INTEGER) [PK, NOT NULL]
  ChainID (INTEGER)  e.g. 13, 6
  Country (TEXT)  e.g. 'CZE', 'SVK'
  Segment (TEXT)  e.g. 'Value for money', 'Premium'

    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).

    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname

    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.

    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.

  ```

Turn 3 — FINALIZE (plain text only, never inside a code block):
  FINAL("YOUR SQL HERE")

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.

    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

      "September 2013 refers to 201309"  → WHERE date_col = '201309'  (not LIKE '2013-09%')
      "ratio = count(A) / count(B)"      → SELECT COUNT(CASE WHEN x='A' THEN 1 END)*1.0 / COUNT(CASE WHEN x='B' THEN 1 END)
      "meeting events refers to type = 'Meeting'" → WHERE type = 'Meeting'
  ② If any names, locations, or string values are NOT defined by the Hint,
    use db.sample_values() to see how they are actually stored in the database.

  ```python
  print(db.execute("YOUR SQL HERE"))
  ```

Turn 3 — FINALIZE (plain text only, never inside a code block):
  FINAL("YOUR SQL HERE")

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.

    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)

QUESTION: Among the transactions made in the gas stations in the Czech Republic, how many of them are taken place after 2012/1/1?
⚠️  HINT (follow these definitions EXACTLY):
  Czech Republic can be represented as the Country value in the gasstations table is 'CZE'

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: What was the difference of number of units sold in station number 1 and number 2 on year 2012?
  Hint: station 1 refers to station_nbr = 1; station 2 refers to station_nbr = 2; on year 2012 refers to substring (date, 1, 4) = '2012'; difference = Subtract (Sum(units where station_nbr = 1), Sum(units where station_nbr = 2))
  SQL: SELECT SUM(CASE WHEN T1.station_nbr = 1 THEN units ELSE 0 END) - SUM(CASE WHEN T1.station_nbr = 2 THEN units ELSE 0 END) FROM relation AS T1 INNER JOIN sales_in_weather AS T2 ON T1.store_nbr = T2.store_nbr WHERE T2.`date` LIKE '%2012%'

Schema:
DATABASE: debit_card_specializing

TABLE: customers
  CustomerID (INTEGER) [PK, NOT NULL]
  Segment (TEXT)  e.g. 'SME', 'LAM'
  Currency (TEXT)  e.g. 'EUR', 'CZK'

TABLE: gasstations
  GasStationID (INTEGER) [PK, NOT NULL]
  ChainID (INTEGER)  e.g. 13, 6
  Country (TEXT)  e.g. 'CZE', 'SVK'
  Segment (TEXT)  e.g. 'Value for money', 'Premium'

TABLE: products
  ProductID (INTEGER) [PK, NOT NULL]
  Description (TEXT)  e.g. 'Rucní zadání', 'Nafta'

TABLE: sqlite_sequence
  name (UNKNOWN)  e.g. 'transactions_1k'
  seq (UNKNOWN)  e.g. 1000

TABLE: transactions_1k
  TransactionID (INTEGER) [PK]
  Date (DATE)  e.g. '2012-08-24', '2012-08-23'
  Time (TEXT)  e.g. '09:41:00', '10:03:00'
  CustomerID (INTEGER)  e.g. 31543, 46707
  CardID (INTEGER)  e.g. 486621, 550134
  GasStationID (INTEGER)  e.g. 3704, 656
  ProductID (INTEGER)  e.g. 2, 23
  Amount (INTEGER)  e.g. 28, 18
  Price (REAL)  e.g. 672.64, 430.72

TABLE: yearmonth
  CustomerID (INTEGER) [PK, NOT NULL]
  Date (TEXT) [PK, NOT NULL]
  Consumption (REAL)  e.g. 528.3, 1598.28
  FK: CustomerID → customers.None
  FK: CustomerID → customers.None

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel

  Q: To which segment belongs the customer that made the most orders in April 1994?
  Hint: segment refers to c_mktsegment; customer made the most orders in April 1994 refers to c_custkey where o_orderdate LIKE '1994-04-%' and MAX(COUNT(o_orderkey));
  SQL: SELECT T.c_mktsegment FROM ( SELECT T2.c_mktsegment, COUNT(T1.o_orderkey) AS num FROM orders AS T1 INNER JOIN customer AS T2 ON T1.o_custkey = T2.c_custkey WHERE T1.o_orderdate LIKE '1994-04-%' GROUP BY T1.o_custkey ) AS T ORDER BY T.num DESC LIMIT 1

SIMILAR SOLVED EXAMPLES (different databases — copy the SQL *style* and output-column conventions, not the table names):

  Q: How many times is the number of active apps in the event that happened at 7:50:28 on 2016/5/2 than in the event that happened at 7:41:03 on 2016/5/2?
  Hint: how many times = DIVIDE(SUM(IF(timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:50:28', 1,0)), SUM(IF(timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:41:03',1,0))); active refers to is_active = '1'; at 7:50:28 on 2016/5/2 refers to timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:50:28'; at 7:41:03 on 2016/5/2 refers to timestamp = '2016/5/2 7:41:03';
  SQL: SELECT SUM(IIF(timestamp = '2016-05-02 7:50:28', 1, 0)) / SUM(IIF(timestamp = '2016-05-02 7:41:03', 1, 0)) AS num FROM events AS T1 INNER JOIN app_events AS T2 ON T1.event_id = T2.event_id WHERE T2.is_active = '1'

  Hint: DIVIDE(COUNT(c_name where c_mktsegment = 'AUTOMOBILE' and n_name = 'FRANCE'), COUNT(c_name where n_name = 'FRANCE')) as percentage;
  SQL: SELECT CAST(SUM(IIF(T1.c_mktsegment = 'AUTOMOBILE', 1, 0)) AS REAL) * 100 / COUNT(T1.c_name) FROM customer AS T1 INNER JOIN nation AS T2 ON T1.c_nationkey = T2.n_nationkey WHERE T2.n_name = 'FRANCE'

RULES:
  • NEVER write FINAL() in the same message as a code block.
  • ONE code block per turn.
  • Schema is already provided — no need for get_tables() or get_schema().
  • ⛔ NEVER call FINAL() if your last SQL returned 0 rows — that means your query
    is wrong. Fix the WHERE clause, JOIN condition, or value format and retry.
  • SELECT only the columns the question asks for, in the order mentioned.
    Never add extra columns (no aliases, no COUNT(*) unless asked).
  • If the question asks multiple things ("What is X? Who is Y?" / "state A and B"),
    SELECT every asked item, in the order asked — do not answer only one part.
    "How old is the youngest driver? What is his name?" → SELECT age_expr, forename, surname
  • For superlatives (oldest/highest/best/dumbest) return exactly one row:
    ORDER BY col ASC|DESC LIMIT 1 — never WHERE col = (SELECT MIN/MAX(...)) which returns ties.
  • ONLY when the expected answer is literally yes or no ("Did X...?", "Is Y...?", "Was each...?"),
    SELECT the answer itself as EXACTLY ONE column: IIF(condition, 'YES', 'NO') —
    do not return the matching rows, do not add extra columns.
    Comparison questions ("Are there more X or Y? What is the difference?") are NOT yes/no —
    return the value(s) asked.
  • When using T1/T2 aliases, double-check which table each SELECTed column belongs to
    (races.name vs circuits.name) — alias mix-ups are a top error source.
  • NEVER concatenate columns ("full name" → SELECT forename, surname — two columns,
    not forename || ' ' || surname). Return raw columns.
  • When the Hint spells out a formula (DIVIDE(...), SUBTRACT(...), MULTIPLY(...), "X = A / B"),
    translate it into SQL LITERALLY, term by term — do not substitute your own formula,
    denominator, or filter, even if yours seems more correct.
  • DO NOT deduplicate by default. Do not add DISTINCT, and do not worry about a
    JOIN producing duplicate/repeated rows for COUNT/SUM/AVG — use the raw join
    result as-is. Gold answers are computed this way (COUNT/SUM/AVG over the
    natural join cardinality, including repeats) unless the Hint explicitly says
    "distinct" or "unique". Only add DISTINCT when the Hint or question says so.
  • For conditional aggregation use: SUM(CASE WHEN condition THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)
    or IIF(condition, value, 0) — both work in SQLite.
  • For ratios/percentages: CAST(numerator AS REAL) / denominator * 100
    The denominator must be the TOTAL count of ALL rows in the relevant group,
    NOT just the rows matching the condition.
    ✓ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) * 1.0 / COUNT(*)
    ✗ COUNT(CASE WHEN cond THEN 1 END) / COUNT(CASE WHEN other_cond THEN 1 END)
  • For "rank X by Y" questions use a window function AND include the ranked-by
    metric column itsel
